### CHAPTER XIX.
---

<term>Suta<note>The traditional storyteller and narrator of the Puranas</note></term> said:—I will now describe the <term>Praneshwara<note>original: "Praneshwara"; literally "Lord of Life," referring to a rite intended to restore life or counteract the fatal effects of poison</note></term> ritual of <term>Garuda<note>The divine eagle-king and the mount of Vishnu; he is the traditional enemy of serpents and a master of anti-venom lore</note></term> as narrated by <term>Shiva<note>The god of destruction and transformation, often associated with the mastery over poisons</note></term>.

I shall first describe the places where a person, bitten by a serpent, does not survive, namely: a funeral pyre, an ant-hill, a well, and the cavity of a tree. A person whose limb-lines are hidden or swollen beyond recognition does not live. A man dies if he is bitten on the sixth day of the lunar fortnight, or when the sun is in the constellations of <term>Cancer<note>original: "Karkata"</note></term> or <term>Aries<note>original: "Mesha"</note></term>, or when the moon resides in the <term>lunar mansions<note>original: "Nakshatra"</note></term> of <term>Mula<note>The 19th lunar mansion, associated with the root or core</note></term>, <term>Magha<note>The 10th lunar mansion, containing five stars</note></term>, or <term>Ashlesha<note>The 9th lunar mansion, often associated with serpents</note></term>. 

A man also dies when he is bitten on the sides, the loins, the throat, the joints, the temple, the ears, or the belly. Certain people act as "emissaries of Death"—if the person sent to fetch a physician is a <term>Dandin<note>a staff-bearing hermit or ascetic</note></term>, a person carrying weapons, a beggar, or a naked person, the patient will not survive. Survival is also unlikely if the bite occurs on the mouth, arms, neck, or back.

Every day, the sun acts as the lord over all serpents for half a <term>Yama<note>A watch of three hours, or one-eighth of a day</note></term>. Following this, the six planets take turns ruling over them in six revolutions. During the night, five planets rule over them in five revolutions. Each planet acts as a presiding star for a specific serpent lord:
*   The **Sun** rules over **Sesha** <note>The primordial, thousand-headed serpent</note>
*   The **Moon** rules over **Phani** <note>The hooded serpent</note>
*   **Mars** rules over **Takshaka**
*   **Jupiter** rules over **Karkotyna** <note>Also known as Karkotaka</note>
*   **Venus** rules over **Padma** and **Mahapadma**
*   **Saturn** rules over **Shankha**
*   **Rahu** <note>The shadow planet responsible for eclipses</note> rules over **Kuluka** and **Ahi**.

**Jupiter** represents Death itself during both the day and the night. <note>Traditional lore suggests that if a person is bitten on a Thursday, the day ruled by Jupiter, they cannot survive under any circumstances.</note> **Saturn** represents Death during the day and...

<meta>This page continues the discourse on surviving snakebites by moving from physical signs to astrological timing and ritual cures. It introduces the concept of mapping time onto the human body to diagnose the severity of a bite and provides the Garuda-centric mantras used to repel serpents and neutralize venom.</meta>

-># GARUDA PURANAM<-

<page-num>54</page-num>

During the period when <term>Rahu<note>The "shadow planet" in Vedic astrology responsible for eclipses, usually considered an inauspicious influence</note></term> presides over <term>Kuluka<note>A specific period of the day governed by Rahu, considered highly unlucky for beginning new tasks or for health</note></term> at the hours of the conjunction of two-half <term>Yamas<note>A traditional measure of time; one Yama is approximately three hours</note></term>, the timing is hostile to life.

A day, consisting of sixty <term>dandas<note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes; sixty dandas make up a full 24-hour cycle</note></term>, should be distributed over a human body divided into three sections. Five *dandas* should be assigned to the toe, twelve to the feet, five to the calf, two to the knee and one to the <term>organ of generation<note>The reproductive organs</note></term>. Six *dandas* should be assigned to the navel, four to the breast and eight to the throat. Fifteen *dandas* should be allotted to the tip of the nose and one each to the eye, ear, eye-brow, and temple. Then all the days, beginning with <term>Pratipat<note>The first day of either the waxing or waning lunar fortnight</note></term>, the first day of a fortnight, should be allotted to all the limbs beginning with the head. If the moon lords over the right part of a man's body he does not survive. If it lords over the left part of a woman's body she dies. The benumbed part should be rubbed over with a hand till unconsciousness is removed.

The great <term>mantram<note>A sacred utterance or chant believed to have spiritual or magical power</note></term> of self, pure like crystal, called <term>Hansa<note>Literally "Swan"; in this context, it refers to the "Soham" breath-mantra associated with the soul or life force</note></term>, is to be known as one which counteracts the evil effect of poisoning. Its <term>Veeja<note>original: "Veeja" (Bija); a "seed" syllable that contains the essence of a mantra’s power</note></term> is of four sorts:

->**Om, Kuru, Kunde Swāhā.**<-

Formerly this learning was kept by <term>Garuda<note>The divine eagle-king and mount of Lord Vishnu, famous for his enmity toward serpents</note></term> for protecting the three worlds. Desirous of killing the serpents, he assigned the <term>Pranava<note>The sacred syllable "Om," considered the primordial sound of the universe</note></term> to his mouth. An intelligent worshipper should assign *Kuru* to his throat, *Kunda* to the calves, and *Swaha* to the two feet. This <term>Nyasa<note>The ritual practice of touching various parts of the body while reciting specific parts of a mantra to "install" divine power within oneself</note></term> is called *Yugaha*. The serpents leave the house in which this mantram is written. Having recited it a thousand times, one should place a thread on his ear. The serpents leave the house in which sugar, after reciting this mantram, is thrown. By reciting it seven <term>lacs<note>A unit in the Indian numbering system equal to one hundred thousand (100,000)</note></term> of times, the celestials and <term>Asuras<note>A class of powerful beings in Hindu mythology, often translated as "demons" or "titans," who compete with the gods</note></term> obtain <term>Siddhi<note>Supernatural powers or spiritual perfection attained through disciplined practice</note></term>.

->**Om, Swaha unto golden lines, unto him having the form of a fowl.**<-

In this way two letters should be written on each petal of a—

---

* One sixtieth part of day and night. Twenty four minutes.

**Suta** <note>The narrator of the Puranas</note> said:—I will now describe the highly secret <term>mantras</term> narrated by **Shiva**. His weapons are the noose, the bow, the discus, the club, the dart, and the <term>Pattisha<note>A traditional Indian spear or battle-axe with a trident-like or broad blade</note></term>. Having used these weapons, once they are empowered with <term>mantras</term> in a battle, a king conquers his enemies. The <term>mantra</term> used for purifying other <term>mantras</term> should first be written on a lotus petal. 

**Om** is the <term>seed-syllable of Brahma<note>Original: "Brahma Vijam"; a Bija or seed-syllable is a single-syllable sound that contains the essential power of a deity</note></term>.  
**Hrim** is the <term>seed-syllable of Vishnu<note>Original: "Vishnu Vijam"</note></term>.  

These three <term>seed-syllables</term> should be assigned to the head of **Shiva** three times in proper order:

->**Om, Hrim, Hrim.**<-

Having taken up the dart in his hand, the practitioner should whirl it toward the sky. By seeing this action, all evil stars and serpents are destroyed. Having held the smoky-colored bow in his hand, a man should meditate on it in the sky. By this, the wicked serpents, evil stars, storm clouds, and <term>Rakshasas<note>Powerful, supernatural beings in Hindu mythology, often translated as demons or spirits, capable of changing shape</note></term> are destroyed. This <term>mantra</term> protects the three worlds—what need is there to mention its power in the land of mortals?

->**Om, jum, Sam, Hum, Phat.**<-

Eight sticks of <term>Catechu wood<note>Wood from the Acacia catechu tree, prized for its durability and used in sacred fire rituals</note></term>, empowered with <term>mantras</term>, should be placed on the ground. That will prevent the falling of thunderbolts. The eight sticks should be empowered with the great <term>mantra</term> described...

...enemies by Garuda. <note>The text continues from the previous page, completing the thought that a ruler triumphs over his foes through these sacred practices.</note> The ground should be dug twenty-one times during the night. This will ward off the dangers proceeding from lightning, mice, and thunderbolts.

The <term>mantra<note>A sacred sound or phrase used in meditation or ritual to invoke divine power</note></term> is:—Hara <note>meaning "The Destroyer," a name for Shiva</note>, Kshara <note>meaning "The Perishable"</note>, Amala <note>meaning "The Pure"</note>, Vashat <note>a ritual exclamation used when making an offering</note>, combined with the <term>Vindu<note>Also spelled Bindu; the "seed-point" or dot representing the manifestation of the cosmos, often used as a nasal sound in mantras</note></term> of Sadashiva <note>The eternal, highest form of the god Shiva</note>.

**Om, Hram**, salutation unto Sadashiva.

He should then assign <term>pinda<note>Ritual offerings, typically balls of cooked rice or flour, used to feed spirits or ancestors</note></term> that are as bright and red as <term>Darimi<note>Likely referring to the pomegranate (Dadima) flower, known for its vibrant red color</note></term> flowers using the forefinger. This <term>pinda</term> is called Bhairava <note>named after the terrifying manifestation of Shiva</note>; it counteracts the effects of poison and the evil influences of hostile planets. It protects the field and grinds the goblins and **Rakshasas** to dust.

**Om, Namas** <note>meaning "salutations" or "I bow"</note>. Having meditated on the thunderbolt in his hand, he should ward off the evil influence of wicked clouds; using the <term>Vajra Mudra<note>The "thunderbolt" hand gesture, symbolizing both an irresistible force and the indestructible nature of reality</note></term>, he should ward off all ghosts and enemies who use poison. **Om, Kshum, Namas**. He should meditate on his left hand. It destroys all venomous creatures.

**Om, Hram, Namas**. The very recitation of this <term>mantra</term> destroys evil clouds and unlucky stars. Having meditated on death, he should consume the universe with the weapon of destruction.

**Om, Kshma, Namas**.

Meditating on Bhairava, one should remove the evil influences of stars, goblins, and poison.

**Om lasat, jhvaksha Swāhā** <note>Swāhā is an invocation used when making ritual offerings to a fire</note>. This <term>mantra</term> destroys the enemies of the field—namely the evil stars, goblins, poison, and birds.

**Om Ksham Namas**. The figure of a cistern should be drawn with blood, and then the names of the planets should be written there.

**Om, Death, Death, Kill, Kill, I make this offering.** <note>original: "Om, Mara, Mara, Maraya, Maraya Swāhā."</note> **Om, Hum, Phat, I make this offering.** <note>Ritual syllables used to focus energy and remove obstacles.</note>

With higher energies, the lower ones should be suppressed. ### CHAPTER XXI.

---

**SUTA** <note>The narrator of the Puranas, a traditional storyteller</note> said:—I will now describe separately the adoration of the **Five-Faced One** <note>original: Panchavaktra; a form of Shiva representing his five aspects or "mouths"</note> which yields both worldly enjoyment and spiritual liberation.

**Om, Earth, salutation unto Vishnu, the first being, the support of all, unto him who possesses form, I make this offering.** <note>original: "Om bhur [salutation unto] Vishnu, the first bhuta, the stay of all, unto him having a form, Swāhā."</note>

First of all, the invocation of **The Newly Born** <note>original: Sadyajata; the aspect of Shiva associated with the earth and the western direction</note> should be made with this mantra.

**Om, Ham, salutation unto The Newly Born.** His <term>kalas<note>Divine "parts," manifestations, or energies associated with a deity</note></term> are eight in number, namely: **Success**, **Prosperity**, **Fortitude**, **Fortune**, **Intelligence**, **Splendor**, **Ritual Offering**, and **Stability**. <note>original names: Siddhi, Riddhi, Dhriti, Lakshmi, Medhā, Kānti, Swadhā, Sthiti.</note>

**Om, Ham, salutation unto The Noble Lord.** <note>original: Vāmadeva; the aspect of Shiva associated with water, the north, and preservation</note> His energies are thirteen in number, namely: **Splendor**, **Protection**, **Delight**, **Nourishment**, **Beauty**, **Desire**, **Thought**, **Action**, **Passion**, **Insight**, **Pleasure**, **Terror**, and **Delusion**. <note>original names: Rājā, Rakshā, Rati, Pālyā, Kanti, Trishnā, Mati, Kriyā, Kāmā, Buddhi, Rati, Trāsani, and Mohini.</note>

-># THE GARUDA PURANA<-

<page-num>59</page-num>

**Manonmani** <note>literally "Mind-uplifter"</note>, **Aghorā** <note>"The Non-terrifying"</note>, **Mohā** <note>"Delusion"</note>, **Kshudhā** <note>"Hunger"</note>, **Kala** <note>"Time" or "Destiny"</note>, **Nidrā** <note>"Sleep"</note>, **Mrityu** <note>"Death"</note>, **Maya** <note>"Illusion"</note>—these are the eight dreadful **Kalas** <note>original: "Kalas"; divine energies or divisions of power</note>.

**Om, Hraim**, salutation unto **Tatpurusha** <note>The "Supreme Being," representing the aspect of Shiva facing East</note>. His energies are **Nivrithi** <note>Withdrawal from worldly concerns</note>, **Pratishtha** <note>Foundation or Installation</note>, **Vidyā** <note>Knowledge</note>, **Shanti** <note>Peace</note> and **Kevalā** <note>The Absolute</note>.

**Om, Hroum**, salutation unto **Ishāna** <note>The "Ruler," representing the aspect of Shiva facing Upward</note>. His energies are **Nishchata** <note>Certainty</note>, **Niranjanā** <note>The Untainted</note>, **Shashini** <note>The Radiant like the Moon</note>, **Anganā** <note>The Feminine Form</note>, **Marichi** <note>Ray of Light</note> and **Jvalini** <note>The Flaming One</note>.

---

## CHAPTER XXII.

---

**SUTA** <note>The traditional narrator of the Puranas</note> said :—I will now describe the great adoration of Shiva which yields enjoyment and emancipation. He is of a quiescent soul, present everywhere, void and stationed in a room of twelve parts <note>likely referring to the *dvadashanta*, a spiritual center located twelve finger-breadths above the head or in the heart</note>. His five mouths are represented by five short vowels and his limbs by long vowels added with a <term>Vindu<note>A dot used in Sanskrit writing to represent a nasal sound, symbolizing the concentrated point of all creation</note></term>. His weapon is represented by a <term>Visarga<note>A phonetic mark (ḥ) in Sanskrit representing an aspirated release of breath, symbolizing the outward manifestation of energy</note></term>. Then the word Shiva should be written upwards. With the sixth, the great sacred sound **Houm**, impregnated with various meanings, should be written. With hands the back parts of the feet should be held and then the ends thereof should be placed on the head. This is the great <term>Mudrā<note>A symbolic hand gesture or bodily posture used in ritual to focus and direct energy</note></term>. Then the assignment of hands should be made <note>the ritual process of *Nyasa*, where different parts of the body are touched while reciting mantras to instill them with divine presence</note>. With the **weapon-mantra** <note>original: "astra mantram"; a specific sound formula used for protection, typically ending in 'Phat'</note> the back should be purified. Then beginning with the pinky finger and ending with the forefinger, all the fingers should be assigned.

I will now describe the adoration in the center of the lotus of the heart. One should adore religion, knowledge, disassociation from the world, and prosperity in the heart. The invocation and the installation rites of the Deity, the water for washing feet, and the <term>Arghya<note>An honorary ritual offering of water, flowers, or rice presented to a guest or deity</note></term> should be dedicated to the heart. And similarly, the rinsing of the mouth, sprinkling of water, and adoration should be made.

<page-num>60</page-num>

I will now describe the rites of fire worship. They should be written with the **Mantra of the Weapon** <note>original: "astra mantram"; a specific sacred sound used for protection and the dismissal of negative influences</note>. Then the **spiritual coat of mail** <note>a protective energetic barrier visualized around the body</note> should be sprinkled with water. And the **Divine Energy** <note>original: "Sakti"</note> should be assigned to the heart. Then he should place fire either in the heart or in the ritual pit for the **Divine Energy**. Having performed the **Ritual of Conception** <note>original: "Garbhādhāna"; here referring to a symbolic purification rite to "birth" the sacred fire</note> he should celebrate the other rites. Afterwards, having assigned to the heart the freedom from all those rites as [well as] all other rites, he should perform the **Fire Sacrifice** <note>original: "Homa"</note> for **Shiva** and all the members of his divine family. Then, in a diagram having the figure of a lotus drawn inside it, he should worship the bull-emblemed deity **Shambhu** <note>an epithet of Shiva meaning "The Benevolent"</note>.

### CHAPTER XXIII.

---

**SUTA** <note>The traditional narrator of the Puranas</note> said:—I will describe the adoration of **Shiva** by which religious profit, desire, and so forth are secured. With three **mantras** <note>sacred chants</note> beginning with **Om** and ending with **May it be offered** <note>original: "Swāhā"; the ritual exclamation used when casting offerings into the fire</note>, the mouth should be rinsed with water.

**Om, Ham**, salutation unto the principle of self. **Hrim** <note>original OCR: "Hirm"; a "seed" mantra associated with the heart and the Goddess</note>, salutation unto the principle of learning. **Om, Hum**, salutation unto the principle of Shiva, **May it be offered**. The ears should be closed with the heart <note>likely referring to a meditative gesture or internal focus</note>.

**Om, ham, yam, Swaha**, are the mantras for bathing with ashes and offering oblations of water. All the gods and all the **Sages** <note>original: "Munis"</note> should be adored with the mantra "Salutation **bhoushat**" <note>a ritual exclamation used to invoke or energize a mantra</note>.

All the **Ancestors** <note>original: "Pitris"</note> and all the **Grandfathers** <note>original: "Pitamahas"; also referring to the ancient patriarchs</note> should be adored with mantras ending with the word **Swadhā** <note>the specific ritual call used when making offerings to deceased ancestors</note>.

**Om, Ham**, salutation unto the great-grandfathers. The same is for the maternal grandfathers. [The next is] **Ham**,

salutations unto all the **Divine Mothers** <note>original: "Matris"; a group of goddesses representing the creative and protective energies of the great gods</note>. Then the vital airs should be suppressed <note>the practice of breath control known as prāṇāyāma</note>. Then he should rinse his mouth with water, rub his body and recite the **Gayatri** <note>a sacred Vedic prayer for enlightenment</note> [which is as follows]:

“Om, Ham, let us contemplate the **Great Lord** <note>original: "Maheshaya"</note>; let us meditate on the **Purifier of Speech** <note>original: "Vāgvishudhaya"</note>; may that **Rudra** <note>the fierce, howling form of Shiva</note> inspire us.”

He should adore lightnings with the sound *Ram* in the east and other quarters, *rom* in the middle, and *ran* on all sides. He should adore the seat of the sun, the form of the sun, and the sun <unclear>itself</unclear> with the mantra “Hrām, Hrum, Sas.”

**Om** and **am** are the mantras for the heart of the sun and his head and tuft of hair. **Roum** is for heaven, sky and earth. The burning mantra **Hum** is for the spiritual coat of mail; the weapon mantra <note>original: "astram"</note> is for the initiated queen. The worshipper should adore all in the heart of the sun.

**Som** is the mantra for the **Moon** <note>original: "Soma"</note>, **Man** for **Mars** <note>original: "Mangala"</note>, **Vam** for **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note>, **Vrim** for **Jupiter** <note>original: "Vrihaspati"</note>, **bham** for **Venus** <note>original: "Bhargava"</note>, **Jum** for **Saturn** <note>original: "Shani"</note> and **ram** for **Rahu** <note>the celestial shadow that causes eclipses</note>. With **Ram** one should adore **Ketu** <note>the descending node of the moon, often depicted as a comet or tail</note> and with **Om** the solar disc.

Having adored the sun and <unclear>rinsed</unclear> his mouth with water, he should assign all the fingers beginning with the youngest <note>a ritual called 'kara-nyāsa', where the practitioner mentally places divine energy into their hands</note>.

**Ham**, **Him** is the mantra for the head, **Hum** for the **sacred tuft of hair** <note>original: "Sikhā"</note>, **Haim** for the coat of mail, **Houm** for the eyes, and **Hos** for the weapon. Having thus placed the **Divine Energy** <note>original: "Sakti"</note> he—

One should perform again the **ritual placement of mantras** <note>original: "nyāsa"; the practice of touching body parts while reciting sacred sounds to infuse them with divinity</note> for the **purification of the elements** <note>original: "bhuta-suddhi"; a meditative process to cleanse the physical body</note>.

Then, preparing a vessel for **honorific offerings** <note>original: "arghya"; a respectful offering usually consisting of water, flowers, or grain</note>, he should sprinkle it with water and then adore the self as being stationed in the lotus [of the heart]. In the exterior, he should use the mantra “Salutation unto **Shiva** <note>the Auspicious One</note>,” then honor **Nandi** <note>the bull who serves as Shiva's mount</note> and **Mahākāla** <note>the Great Time-Lord, a guardian deity</note> at the gate. Next, he honors **Ganga** and **Yamuna** <note>the personifications of the sacred rivers</note>, the goddess of speech, the **mystic mark on the chest** <note>original: "Srivatsa"; a symbol of good fortune</note>, the presiding goddess of the land, **Brahmā** <note>the Creator god</note>, **Gana** <note>the leader of attendants, likely Ganesha</note>, the preceptor, **Power** <note>original: "Sakti"</note>, and the **Infinite Serpent** <note>original: "Ananta"</note> in the middle of the lotus-seed vessel. He should place **Virtue** <note>original: "Dharma"</note> and other qualities in the East and other directions, and **Vice** <note>original: "Adharma"</note> and others in the south-east part of the lotus. Then **Yama** <note>the Lord of Death</note> and **Jejestha** <note>an elder goddess</note> in the middle, and **Roudri** <note>the Fierce One</note>, **Kāli** <note>the Dark One</note>, and **Shivāsita** <note>the White-Shiva goddess</note> should be honored in the eastern part of the lotus of the heart.

Then the **Goddess who Disturbs Strength** <note>original: "Valavikarini"</note> should be adored with the mantra “Om, Houm, salutation unto Valavikarini.” The goddess of strength, which subdues all creatures, should be next adored. Then, on the ritual seat before Shiva, the **Goddess who Directs the Mind** <note>original: "Manonmani"</note> should be adored. He should then make a seat for Shiva and his great image. He should invoke the spirit of Shiva within the image. He should then perform the rites of **Installation** <note>original: "Sthapanam"</note>, **Invocation of Presence** <note>original: "Sannidhana"</note>, **Restraint** <note>original: "Nirodha"</note>, **Making the Image Whole** <note>original: "Sakalikarana"</note>, **Hand Gestures** <note>original: "Mudra"</note>, **Honorific Water** <note>original: "Arghya"</note>, **Water for Feet** <note>original: "Padya"</note>, **Water for Rinsing** <note>original: "Achama"</note>, **Anointing with Oil** <note>original: "Abhyanga"</note>, **Rubbing with Fragrant Paste** <note>original: "Udvarta"</note>, **Bathing** <note>original: "Snāna"</note>, and **Wiping/Cleaning** <note>original: "Nirmanchana"</note>. 

Next he should offer garments, ointments, flowers, incense, lamps, **sacrificial food** <note>original: "charu"; a mixture of rice and milk</note>, water for rinsing the mouth, a delightful habitation, betel nut, an umbrella, **fly-whisks** <note>original: "chowries"</note>, and the **sacred thread**. He should then imagine the form of the one God, recite his name and dedicate the ritual to him. With songs of praise, bowing, and meditation in the heart, this adoration of the **Name and Limbs** <note>original: "Nāmānga"</note> should be finished. **Agnisha** <note>the deity of fire</note> should be placed in the north-west. He should then adore **Indra** <note>the King of the Gods</note> and other deities, along with **Chanda** <note>the fierce guardian of Shiva’s offerings</note>, and dedicate to them offerings, saying: “Thou art the mystery of mysteries and the protector. Do thou accept this my recitation. O God, may I, by thy favor and on thy being present here, attain success. O god! O thou the giver of fame! do thou—”

> “…destroy all my actions, good or bad, whatever I have done, I who possess the dignity of **Shiva** <note>the Auspicious One; the supreme deity of this rite</note>. I shall now describe another method for the adoration of Shiva. **Gana** <note>the leader of the divine attendants, often identified with Ganesha</note>, **Saraswati** <note>the goddess of knowledge and arts</note>, **Nandi** <note>the sacred bull and gatekeeper of Shiva</note>, **Mahākāla** <note>the "Great Time," a fierce guardian aspect of Shiva</note>, **Gangā**, **Yamuna** <note>the personified spirits of India's most sacred rivers</note>, and the presiding deity of the house—all these should be assigned to the eastern gate. Having known all these as the powers of Shiva, an emancipated person, possessing the true knowledge of the deity, becomes Shiva himself. [He should also meditate:] 
> “He who is Shiva is **Hari** <note>a name for Vishnu, the Preserver</note> and **Brahmā** <note>the Creator god</note>.”

The mantra is: "Hrim <note>a sacred seed-syllable associated with the heart and the Goddess</note>, O **Durga**, protect." Then an assignment of the mantra should be made to the eight **Saktis** <term>divine feminine energies</term>, called the **Chandikas** <note>fierce aspects of the Goddess</note>, namely: **Rudrachanda** <note>Fierce Chanda</note>, **Prachanda** <note>Terrible Chanda</note>, **Chandogra** <note>Violent Chanda</note>, **Chandanayika** <note>Leader Chanda</note>, **Chanda** <note>The Fierce One</note>, **Chandavati** <note>Possessing Fierceness</note>, and **Chandarupa** <note>Fierce-formed</note> with the mantra, "O **Durga**, O **Durga**, O protectress." Then **Sadasiva** <note>the Eternal Shiva</note>, the lord of spirits and the lotus seat, should be adored.

*Em, Klim* <note>seed-syllables for wisdom and desire</note>, salutation unto **Shri Tripura** <note>The Goddess of the Three Cities; the text reads "Sonstripura," likely a transcription error for "Shri Tripura"</note>. *Om, Hram, Hrim, Kshem, Kshaim, Strim, Skom, Rom, Sphem, Sphom* <note>these are Bija or "seed" mantras—phonetic sounds used to invoke specific spiritual vibrations</note>, the lotus seat and the heart, etc., of **Tripura**.

Then upon the lotus **pitha** <term>altar or sacred seat</term> should be adored **Brahmani** <note>the power of the Creator</note>, **Maheshwari** <note>the power of Shiva</note>, **Kaumari** <note>the power of the war-god Kartikeya</note>, **Vaishnavi** <note>the power of Vishnu</note>, **Varahi** <note>the power of the divine Boar</note>, **Indradevata** <note>the power of the king of gods</note>, **Chamunda** <note>the slayer of the demons Chanda and Munda</note>, and **Chandika**. Then the **Bhairavas** <note>fierce, terrifying forms of Shiva</note> should be adored. **Asitanga**, **Ruru**, **Chanda**, **Krodha**, **Unmattabhairava**, **Kapali**, **Bhishana**, and **Samhara** are the eight **Bhairavas**.<gloss>These are the personifications of the terrific power of Shiva.</gloss>

The practitioner should meditate in his heart, within a **mandala** <term>sacred geometric diagram</term> containing a lotus and having three corners, upon **Rati** <note>Goddess of Pleasure</note>, **Priti** <note>Goddess of Affection</note>, **Kamadeva** <note>the God of Love</note>, the five arrows <note>the floral arrows of desire</note>, the **Yoginis** <note>female semi-divine spirits</note>, **Valuka**, **Durga**, **Vighnaraja** <note>Ganesha, the Lord of Obstacles</note>, the **Guru** <note>the spiritual preceptor</note>, and the **Kshatrapa** <note>the protector of the region</note>.

By reciting this mantra a *lac* <note>one hundred thousand</note> of times and by offering oblations to the fire, **Tripura** grants **Siddhi** <term>spiritual perfection or the attainment of supernatural powers</term>.

### CHAPTER XXVI.

---

SUTA <note>the narrator and sage</note> said:—Thereupon <term>Karanyasa</term> <note>the ritual "assignment" or projection of sacred syllables onto the hands</note> and the rite of purification should be performed. Vishnu, the <term>Saktis</term> <note>the divine feminine energies or powers of a deity</note>, the tortoise <note>the Kurma avatar who supports the world</note>, <term>Ananta</term> <note>the infinite celestial serpent</note>, earth, religion, knowledge, and disassociation from the world. By reciting his name and adoring him one attains all desired-for objects.

> Om, Hrim, Shrim, Klim, Hum, Om, Salutation <note>original: "Namas"</note>: O foremost of male beings! O you of an incomparable form! O you, the abode of the goddess of prosperity! O you, the agitator of the entire universe! O you who pierce the hearts of all women! O you who drive the three worlds to madness! Agitate the hearts of all beautiful women among the gods and the <term>Asuras</term> <note>divine beings often characterized as the rivals or "demons" of the gods</note>; dry them up, strike them, restrain them, melt them, and attract them. O you of great auspiciousness! O you of good fortune! O you, the giver of all desired objects! Destroy such and such a person with your discus, club, or sword. All the <term>Trailokyamohana</term> <note>meaning "Enchanting the Three Worlds"</note> <term>mantrams</term> <note>sacred ritual chants or formulas</note> are capable of securing the accomplishment of all objects. They may be meditated upon again separately and in brief.

Having adored with <term>mantrams</term> the seat, the image, the six limbs, the discus, the club, the sword, the mace, the conch-shell, the <term>Sharnga</term> <note>Vishnu’s celestial bow</note>, the arrow, the noose, the goad, <term>Lakshmi</term> <note>the goddess of prosperity</note>, <term>Garuda</term> <note>the divine eagle-mount of Vishnu</note>, and <term>Vishwaksena</term> <note>the commander-in-chief of Vishnu’s divine attendants</note>, one attains to all things.

Having recited it one hundred and eight times he should dedicate it. [To the] <term>Sharnga</term> bow <note>original: "Shrānga"; the celestial bow of Lord Vishnu</note>, unto the giver of prosperity. Salutation again and again unto the dearest lord of <term>Shree</term> <note>the goddess Lakshmi, representing fortune and beauty</note> of a quiescent soul, unto <term>Shriman</term> <note>the possessor of glory and fortune</note>. Salutation unto the abode of Shri mountain, unto him who does us good. Salutation again and again unto the mass of spiritual well-being. Salutation again and again unto <term>Shrikara</term> <note>the granter of prosperity</note>. Salutation again and again unto him who is worthy of adoration and being taken refuge with.

Having thus chanted the hymn, bowed unto him the worshipper should perform the <term>Visarjana</term> <note>original: "Visarjana"; the ritual dismissal or "sending away" of the deity’s presence at the conclusion of a ceremony</note> (the life-destroying rite) <note>here referring to the ritual withdrawal of the life-force previously invoked into the idol</note> of the god of gods.

O <term>Rudra</term> <note>a name for the god Shiva in his fierce aspect</note>, I have thus described the adoration of the great Vishnu. He, who does it with great reverential faith, attains to the great station. He, who reads this chapter describing the adoration of Vishnu, has all his sins washed off, and attains to the exalted station of Vishnu.

---

->### CHAPTER XXXI.<-

---

**RUDRA said:**—O lord, do thou again describe unto me the adoration of the lord of the universe by which I may cross over the most difficult ocean of <term>Samsara</term> <note>the eternal cycle of birth, death, and rebirth</note> (transmigratory series.)

**HARI said:**—O bull-emblemed deity <note>a reference to Shiva, whose vehicle and emblem is the bull Nandi</note>, hear, O great one, I will describe the adoration of the Lord Vishnu which yields the most auspicious emancipation and enjoyment.

Having bathed and performed the rite of <term>Sandhyā</term> <note>the essential daily prayers performed at the junctions of the day, such as sunrise and sunset</note> a worshipper should enter the sacrificial room. Having washed hands and feet and rinsed his mouth particularly he should assign the words of the principal <term>mantram</term> <note>a sacred sound, syllable, or phrase used as a spiritual tool</note> to his hand. Hear, hear, O Rudra, I will describe the principal mantram of the deity.

**Om, Shrim, Hrim <note>Original: "Om, Śrīm, Hrīm"; these are "Bija" or seed-mantras associated with auspiciousness and the heart energy</note>, salutation unto Shridhara <note>An epithet of Vishnu meaning "the Bearer of Prosperity"</note> Vishnu.**

This is the <term>mantram</term> <note>a sacred utterance or prayer</note> of the Lord Vishnu, the king of the celestials. It destroys all diseases, counteracts the evil influence of the planets, dissipates all sins, and gives enjoyment and emancipation. A learned worshipper should then make the assignment of the limbs <note>the ritual of <term>Nyāsa</term>, where the practitioner "places" specific divine energies on different parts of their own body through touch and prayer</note>:

**Om, Ham <note>a seed-sound for space/ether</note>, salutation unto the heart. Om, Him, salutation unto the head, Swāhā <note>a ritual exclamation for offerings</note>. Om, Hum, salutation unto the tuft of hair, Vashat. Om, Haim, salutation unto the coat of mail, Hum. Om, Houm, salutation unto the three eyes, Voushat. Om, Has, salutation unto the weapon, phat.**

I have thus described unto thee the mantram of the powerful Vishnu. Having performed <term>Nyāsa</term> (the ritual assignment of mantrams to the body), a self-controlled worshipper should make <term>Mudras</term> <note>symbolic or ritual hand gestures</note>. Having meditated upon the thought “I am Vishnu,” he should perform the purificatory rite. With the mantrams <italic>yam</italic>, <italic>Ksham</italic>, and <italic>Ram</italic>, he should mentally harden and form an egg <note>representing the ritual creation of a protected spiritual space or body</note> and then cut it with the <term>Pranava</term> <note>the sacred syllable "Om"</note>. 

Then, O bull-emblemed deity <note>a title for Shiva, whose vehicle is the bull Nandi</note>, thinking of the form as described before, he should make the worship of self with sacred sweet-scented flowers. Then, invoking the spirit into all the deities seated there, he should worship them with mantrams. Hear them, O <term>Mahādeva</term> <note>"Great God," a name for Shiva</note>! O <term>Shankara</term> <note>"The Beneficent," another name for Shiva</note>!

# CHAPTER XXXII.

**MAHESHWARA** <note>The "Great Lord," a title for Shiva</note> said:—O holder of the conch-shell, the discus, and the club <note>These are the traditional symbolic items held by the four-armed Vishnu</note>, please describe the adoration of the five <term>Tattwas</term> <note>The five essential principles or "realities." A footnote in the source identifies these here as specific ingredients used in Tantric worship</note>. By understanding these with discrimination, a person can reach the most exalted spiritual state.

**Hari** <note>A common name for Vishnu, meaning "The Remover of Sorrows"</note> said:—O **Shankara** <note>"The Beneficent," a name for Shiva</note>! O you of sacred vows! I shall describe the adoration of the five <term>Tattwas</term>, a practice that brings good fortune and is itself auspicious, heavenly, secret, and great, granting all desired objects. Listen, O **Mahādeva** <note>The "Great God," Shiva</note>, to this most sacred subject which destroys the sins of the **Kali** age <note>In Hindu cosmology, the current age of spiritual decline and darkness</note>.

**Vāsudeva** <note>The supreme consciousness, often identified with the person of Krishna or Vishnu</note> is the One—undecaying, peaceful, the Great Soul, eternal, and unstained. O God, through his <term>Māyā</term> <note>The divine power of illusion or the creative energy that manifests the universe</note>, **Hari** exists in five forms: as **Vishnu** who favors the creation and destroys the wicked, and as **Vāsudeva**, **Sangkarshana**, **Pradyumna**, and **Aniruddha** <note>These represent the four "Vyuha" or emanations of the divine consciousness in Vaishnava philosophy</note>. In his own essential form, **Nārāyana** <note>The Supreme Being who rests on the cosmic waters</note> exists as these five. O bull-emblemed deity <note>A reference to Shiva, whose mount is the bull Nandi</note>, listen to the <term>mantrams</term> <note>Sacred phonetic formulas or prayers used in meditation and ritual</note> that express these five forms.

"Om, am, salutation unto **Vāsudeva**. Om, ām, salutation unto **Sankarshana**. Om, am, salutation unto **Pradyumna**. Om, salutation unto **Aniruddha**. Om, salutation unto **Nārāyana**."

I have thus shared the five <term>mantrams</term> that represent these five deities. They are holy and capable of destroying all sins and diseases. Now, O **Shankara**, I will describe the most auspicious way to adore these five <term>Tattwas**—the religious rules and the sacred chants involved, O **Shankara**.

A worshipper should first of all bathe and then perform the **Sandhya** <note>A daily ritual performed at dawn, noon, and sunset, involving prayers and water offerings</note> rite. Then, entering the temple of worship, he should wash his head, rinse his mouth, and then sit in a *baddha* <note>A "bound" or locked yogic sitting posture intended to steady the body for meditation</note> posture. Then, he should recite the <term>mantram</term> "Am, Kshroum, Ram"...
